448.9668pt},G).

.

We should remark that we have dropped the index
d
as this should not cause any confusion for the rest of the document. Recall that we view
G
via its diagonal embedding into
G\times G
. The corollary above is an affinoid version of
[
32
, Corollary 4.5]
.

6
Global sections under affinoid pullback

Throughout this section, we aim to compute global sections under the affinoid pullback defined in Remark
4.21
. We proceed by developing some machinery.

6.1
Preliminary lemmas

Recall that for any sheaf
R
-modules
\mathcal{F}
on a topological space
Y
, we denote
\hat{\mathcal{F}}:=\lim\limits_{\longleftarrow}\mathcal{F}/\pi^{i}\mathcal{F}
its
\pi
-adic completion and
\mathcal{F}_{K}
the sheaf defined by
\mathcal{F}_{K}(U):=F(U)\underset{R}{\otimes}{}K
for any
U\subset Y
open.

Throughout this section we will freely make use of the following easy result: Let
f:Y\to W
be a map of
R
-schemes and
\mathcal{F}
be a sheaf of
R
-modules on
Y
, then
(f_{*}\mathcal{F})_{K}\cong f_{*}(\mathcal{F}_{K}).

Lemma 6.1
.

Let
j:Y\to W
be a closed embedding of
R
-varieties and let
(\mathcal{F}_{i})
be an inverse system of sheaves of
R
-modules such that
\mathcal{F}_{i}/\pi^{i-1}\mathcal{F}_{i}\cong\mathcal{F}_{i-1}
for
i\geq 1
. Let
\mathcal{F}:=\lim\limits_{\longleftarrow}\mathcal{F}_{i}
. Then:

j_{*}\mathcal{F}\cong(\lim\limits_{\longleftarrow}j_{*}\mathcal{F}_{i}).

Proof.

Notice that apriori it is not clear that the right-hand side is well defined. However, since
j
is a closed embedding, it is in particular right exact. Therefore, one can prove in a similar fashion to Lemma
4.3
that

j_{*}\mathcal{F}_{i}/\pi^{i-1}j_{*}\mathcal{F}_{i}\cong j_{*}\mathcal{F}_{i-1}.

Let
\mathcal{G}:=\lim\limits_{\longleftarrow}j_{*}\mathcal{F}_{i}
. Since
j_{*}
is a right adjoint functor to the inverse image functor,
j_{*}
commutes with inverse limits, thus

j_{*}\mathcal{F}=j_{*}(\lim\limits_{\longleftarrow}\mathcal{F}_{i})\cong\lim\limits_{\longleftarrow}(j_{*}\mathcal{F}_{i})=\mathcal{G}.\qed

Lemma 6.2
.

Let
Y
be an
R
-variety and let
p_{r}:Y\times Y\to Y
be the projection on the right factor, and
\mathcal{F}
a sheaf of
R
-modules. Then:

\widehat{p_{r}^{-1}\mathcal{F}}\cong p_{r}^{-1}\hat{\mathcal{F}}.

Proof.

First, notice that there is a map from the right hand side to the left-hand side via the maps
\hat{\mathcal{F}}\to\mathcal{\hat{F}}/\pi^{i}\mathcal{\hat{F}}\cong\mathcal{F}/\pi^{i}\mathcal{F}
. Let
U,V\subset Y
be affine open, so that
U\times V\subset Y\times Y
is affine open. Then, we have:

\begin{split}\widehat{p_{r}^{-1}\mathcal{F}}(U\times V)&=\lim\limits_{\longleftarrow}(p_{r}^{-1}\mathcal{F}/\pi^{i}p_{r}^{-1}\mathcal{F})(U\times V)\\
&\cong\lim\limits_{\longleftarrow}p_{r}^{-1}\mathcal{F}(U\times V)/\pi^{i}p_{r}^{-1}\mathcal{F}(U\times V)\\
&\cong\lim\limits_{\longleftarrow}\mathcal{F}(V)/\pi^{i}\mathcal{F}(V)\\
&\cong\hat{\mathcal{F}}(V)\\
&\cong p_{r}^{-1}\hat{\mathcal{F}}(U\times V).\end{split}

(39)

Now let
(U_{i})_{i\in I}
be an affine open covering of
Y
. Then
\{U_{j}\times U_{k}|j,k\in I\}
is an affine open covering of
Y\times Y
. By equation (
39
), we have that the sheaves
\widehat{p_{r}^{-1}\mathcal{F}}
and
p_{r}^{-1}\hat{\mathcal{F}}
agree on affine open cover and since there exists a map between the two, they are isomorphic.
∎

6.2
Simplyfing the pullback functor

We retain the notation from the previous section. Further, for the rest of the section we assume
\lambda,\mu:\pi^{n}\mathfrak{h}\to R
are
R
-linear
dominant weights
. Since
\mathcal{D}_{n}^{\lambda,\mu}
is a sheaf of
\pi^{n}
-deformed
G
-htdo by Corollary
2.21
and the double flag variety
X\times X
is quasi-compact we have by Theorem
2.17
, Proposition
4.9
and Theorem
4.20
equivalences of categories:

\begin{split}&i_{l}^{\#}:\Coh(\mathcal{D}^{\lambda,\mu}_{n},G)\to\Coh(\mathcal{D}^{\lambda}_{n},B),\\
&\hat{i_{l}}^{\#}:\Coh(\widehat{\mathcal{D}^{\lambda,\mu}_{n}},G)\to\Coh(\widehat{\mathcal{D}^{\lambda}_{n}},B),\\
&\hat{i}_{l,K}^{\#}:\Coh(\widehat{\mathcal{D}^{\lambda,\mu}_{n,K}},G)\to\Coh(\widehat{\mathcal{D}^{\lambda}_{n,K}},B).\end{split}

(40)

We let
i:eB\to X
and
p_{r}:X\times X\to X
denote the natural inclusion and projection onto the right factor, respectively. We also define
\mathcal{M}^{\mu}_{n}:=i_{*}R\underset{\mathcal{O}_{X}}{\otimes}{}\mathcal{D}^{\mu}_{n}.

We can now give a description of the pullback of
G
-equivariant coherent
\widehat{\mathcal{D}^{\lambda,\mu}_{n}}
-modules and
\widehat{\mathcal{D}^{\lambda,\mu}_{n,K}}
-modules. We start by proving
\pi
-adic and affinoid versions of
[
32
, Corollary 6.4]
.

Proposition 6.3
.

Let
\mathcal{M}\in\Coh(\widehat{\mathcal{D}^{\lambda,\mu}_{n}},G)
. Then:

i_{l_{*}}(\hat{i_{l}}^{\#}\mathcal{M})\cong p_{r}^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{M}^{\mu}_{n}}\underset{p_{r}^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{D}^{\mu}_{n}}}{\otimes}{}\mathcal{M}.

Proof.

We know by construction that
\mathcal{M}/\pi^{i}\mathcal{M}\in\Coh(\mathcal{D}^{\lambda,\mu}_{n},G)
. We have

\begin{split}i_{l_{*}}(\hat{i_{l}}^{\#}\mathcal{M})&=i_{l_{*}}(\lim\limits_{\longleftarrow}i_{l}^{\#}(\mathcal{M}/\pi^{i}\mathcal{M}))\\
&\cong\lim\limits_{\longleftarrow}(i_{l_{*}}(i_{l}^{\#}(\mathcal{M}/\pi^{i}\mathcal{M})))\text{ (by Lemma \ref{d3completiondirectimage}) }\\
&\cong\lim\limits_{\longleftarrow}(p_{r}^{-1}\mathcal{M}^{\mu}_{n}\underset{p_{r}^{-1}\mathcal{D}^{\mu}_{n}}{\otimes}{}\mathcal{M}/\pi^{i}\mathcal{M})\text{ (by \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Sta2}{}{}, Corollary 6.4]})}\\
&\cong\lim\limits_{\longleftarrow}(p_{r}^{-1}\mathcal{M}^{\mu}_{n}/\pi^{i}p_{r}^{-1}\mathcal{M}^{\mu}_{n}\underset{p_{r}^{-1}\mathcal{D}^{\mu}_{n}/\pi^{i}p_{r}^{-1}\mathcal{D}^{\mu}_{n}}{\otimes}{}\mathcal{M}/\pi^{i}\mathcal{M})\\
&\cong\widehat{p_{r}^{-1}\mathcal{M}^{\mu}_{n}}\underset{\widehat{p_{r}^{-1}\mathcal{D}^{\mu}_{n}}}{\otimes}{}\mathcal{M}\text{ (by Lemma \ref{d3inversesystemtensor}) }\\
&\cong p_{r}^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{M}^{\mu}_{n}}\underset{p_{r}^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{D}^{\mu}_{n}}}{\otimes}{}\mathcal{M}\text{ (by Lemma \ref{d3inverseimagecompletion})}.\qed\end{split}

(41)

Corollary 6.4
.

Let
\mathcal{M}\in\Coh(\widehat{\mathcal{D}^{\lambda,\mu}_{n,K}},G)
. Then:

i_{l_{*}}(\hat{i}_{l,K}^{\#}\mathcal{M})\cong(p_{r}^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{M}^{\mu}_{n}})_{K}\underset{p_{r}^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{D}^{\mu}_{n,K}}}{\otimes}{}\mathcal{M}.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{M}_{0}
be the correspoding lattice for
\mathcal{M}
such that
\mathcal{M}_{0}\in\Coh(\widehat{\mathcal{D}^{\lambda,\mu}_{n}},G)
. By definition
(\hat{i}_{l,K}^{\#}\mathcal{M})=(\hat{i_{l}}^{\#}\mathcal{M}_{0})_{K}
. We have

\begin{split}i_{l_{*}}(\hat{i}_{l,K}^{\#}\mathcal{M})&=i_{l_{*}}((\hat{i_{l}}^{\#}\mathcal{M}_{0})_{K})\\
&\cong(i_{l_{*}}i_{l}^{\#}\mathcal{M}_{0})_{K}\\
&\cong(p_{r}^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{M}^{\mu}_{n}}\underset{p_{r}^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{D}^{\mu}_{n}}}{\otimes}{}\mathcal{M}_{0})_{K}\text{ (by Proposition \ref{d3piadicpullback}) }\\
&\cong(p_{r}^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{M}^{\mu}_{n}})_{K}\underset{(p_{r}^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{D}^{\mu}_{n}})_{K}}{\otimes}{}(\mathcal{M}_{0})_{K}\\
&\cong(p_{r}^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{M}^{\mu}_{n}})_{K}\underset{(p_{r}^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{D}^{\mu}_{n}})_{K}}{\otimes}{}\mathcal{M}\\
&\cong(p_{r}^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{M}^{\mu}_{n}})_{K}\underset{p_{r}^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{D}^{\mu}_{n,K}}}{\otimes}{}\mathcal{M}.\qed\end{split}

(42)

We should remark that the argument above proves that the functor
\hat{i}_{l,K}^{\#}
is well defined, i.e. it does not depend on the lattice of
\mathcal{M}
.

6.3
Computation of global sections

We aim to compute
\Gamma(X,\hat{i}_{l,K}^{\#}\mathcal{M})
for
\mathcal{M}\in\Coh(\widehat{\mathcal{D}^{\lambda,\mu}_{n,K}},G)
. Recall that
Z=X\times X
denotes the double flag variety. We start by making some notations.

To simplify the notation, we denote
\mathcal{A}:=\Gamma(X,\widehat{\mathcal{D}^{\mu}_{n}})
, so that
\mathcal{A}_{K}=\mathcal{A}\underset{R}{\otimes}{}K\cong\Gamma(X,\widehat{\mathcal{D}^{\mu}_{n,K}})
. Further, we let
\mathcal{B}:=\Gamma(X,\widehat{\mathcal{D}^{\lambda,\mu}_{n}})
. We have by combining Proposition
5.16
and
[
2
, Theorem 6.10b)]
that
\hbox{\hskip 448.9668pt\hskip-24.19533pt\hbox{$U(\mathfrak{{g\times g}})_{n,K}^{\lambda,\mu}$}\hskip-24.19533pt\hskip-448.9668pt\raisebox{19.49014pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.10002pt}{\resizebox{53331012}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-792.00986pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-792.00986pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-448.9668pt\hskip 448.9668pt}\cong\mathcal{B}_{K}\cong\hbox{\hskip 448.9668pt\hskip-17.80641pt\hbox{$U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}^{\lambda}$}\hskip-17.80641pt\hskip-448.9668pt\raisebox{19.49014pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.10002pt}{\resizebox{53331012}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-792.00986pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-792.00986pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-448.9668pt\hskip 448.9668pt}\hat{\underset{K}{\otimes}{}}\mathcal{A}_{K}
. Given a
\mathcal{B}_{K}
-module
M
, we may view it as
\mathcal{A}_{K}
-module via
x.m=(1\otimes x).m
for
x\in\mathcal{A}_{K}
and
m\in M
.

Proposition 6.5
.

\mathcal{M}\in\Coh(\widehat{\mathcal{D}^{\lambda,\mu}_{n,K}},G)
. Then as

U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}^{\lambda}

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

-modules we have an isomorphism:

\Gamma(X,\hat{i}_{l,K}^{\#}\mathcal{M})\cong\Gamma(X,\widehat{\mathcal{M}^{\mu}_{n,K}})\underset{\mathcal{A}_{K}}{\otimes}{}\Gamma(Z,\mathcal{M}).

Proof.

We have
\Gamma(X,\hat{i}_{l,K}^{\#}\mathcal{M})\cong\Gamma(Z,i_{l_{*}}(\hat{i}_{l,K}^{\#}\mathcal{M}))
, so using Corollary
6.4
, it is enough to compute
\Gamma(Z,(p_{r}^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{M}^{\mu}_{n}})_{K}\underset{p_{r}^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{D}^{\mu}_{n,K}}}{\otimes}{}\mathcal{M})
.

We have by the first part of
[
32
, Lemma 6.2]
that there is an exact sequence
(\mathcal{D}^{\mu}_{n})^{a}\to(\mathcal{D}^{\mu}_{n})^{b}\to\mathcal{M}^{\mu}_{n}\to 0
for some
a,b\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
. Since
\pi
-adic completion is exact on coherent modules, we obtain an exact sequence
(\widehat{\mathcal{D}^{\mu}_{n}})^{a}\to(\widehat{\mathcal{D}^{\mu}_{n}})^{b}\to\widehat{\mathcal{M}^{\mu}_{n}}\to 0
, so an exact sequence

(\widehat{\mathcal{D}^{\mu}_{n,K}})^{a}\to(\widehat{\mathcal{D}^{\mu}_{n,K}})^{b}\to\widehat{\mathcal{M}^{\mu}_{n,K}}\to 0.

Since
p_{r}^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{M}^{\mu}_{n,K}}\cong(p_{r}^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{M}^{\mu}_{n}})_{K}
and tensor product is right exact we obtain an exact sequence:

(p_{r}^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{D}^{\mu}_{n,K}})^{a}\underset{p_{r}^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{D}^{\mu}_{n,K}}}{\otimes}{}\mathcal{M}\to(p_{r}^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{D}^{\mu}_{n,K}})^{b}\underset{p_{r}^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{D}^{\mu}_{n,K}}}{\otimes}{}\mathcal{M}\to(p_{r}^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{M}^{\mu}_{n}})_{K}\underset{p_{r}^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{D}^{\mu}_{n,K}}}{\otimes}{}\mathcal{M}\to 0.

To simplify the notation, we let
\mathcal{E}=p_{r}^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{D}^{\mu}_{n,K}}
and
M=\Gamma(Z,\mathcal{M})
. The above short sequence fits into the following commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}^{a}\underset{\mathcal{E}}{\otimes}{}\mathcal{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}^{b}\underset{\mathcal{E}}{\otimes}{}\mathcal{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(p_{r}^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{M}^{\mu}_{n}})_{K}\underset{\mathcal{E}}{\otimes}{}\mathcal{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}^{a}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}^{b}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(p_{r}^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{M}^{\mu}_{n}})_{K}\underset{p_{r}^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{D}^{\mu}_{n,K}}}{\otimes}{}\mathcal{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}

Since
\Gamma(Z,-)
is exact on coherent modules by Theorem
5.7
we obtain a commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma(Z,\mathcal{E}^{a}\underset{\mathcal{E}}{\otimes}{}\mathcal{M})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma(Z,\mathcal{E}^{b}\underset{\mathcal{E}}{\otimes}{}\mathcal{M})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma(Z,(p_{r}^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{M}^{\mu}_{n}})_{K}\underset{\mathcal{E}}{\otimes}{}\mathcal{M})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma(Z,\mathcal{M}^{a})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma(Z,\mathcal{M}^{b})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma(Z,(p_{r}^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{M}^{\mu}_{n}})_{K}\underset{\mathcal{E}}{\otimes}{}\mathcal{M})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M^{a}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M^{b}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma(Z,(p_{r}^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{M}^{\mu}_{n}})_{K}\underset{\mathcal{E}}{\otimes}{}\mathcal{M})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma(X,\widehat{\mathcal{D}^{\mu}_{n,K}}^{a})\underset{\mathcal{A}_{K}}{\otimes}{}M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma(X,\widehat{\mathcal{D}^{\mu}_{n,K}}^{b})\underset{\mathcal{A}_{K}}{\otimes}{}M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma(X,\widehat{\mathcal{M}^{\mu}_{n,K}})\underset{\mathcal{A}_{K}}{\otimes}{}M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}

By construction, we have that the vertical arrows on the first, second and fourth columns are isomorphisms. Considering the first and fourth row and applying the Five Lemma, we get the desired isomorphism.
∎

6.4
Global sections of
\widehat{\mathcal{M}^{\lambda}_{n,K}}

Recall that
\mathfrak{b}^{-}=\mathfrak{n}^{-}\oplus\mathfrak{h}
denotes the negative Borel subalgebra of
\mathfrak{g}
. Let
T(\mu)_{0}=R_{\mu}\underset{U(\mathfrak{b}^{-})_{n}}{\otimes}{}U(\mathfrak{g})_{n}
denote the right
U(\mathfrak{g})_{n}
-module such that
U(\mathfrak{b}^{-})_{n}
acts on
R
via
\mu
. Further, we let
T(\mu):=T(\mu)_{0}\underset{R}{\otimes}{}K\cong K_{\mu}\underset{U(\mathfrak{b}^{-})_{K}}{\otimes}{}U(\mathfrak{g})_{K}
and
\widehat{T(\mu)}:=\widehat{T(\mu)_{0}}\underset{R}{\otimes}{}K\cong T(\mu)\underset{U(\mathfrak{g})_{K}}{\otimes}{}\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}
. We should remark that
T(\mu)
is the same as defined in
[
32
, Lemma 6.2]
.

Proposition 6.6
.

We have
\Gamma(X,\widehat{\mathcal{M}^{\mu}_{n,K}})\cong\widehat{T(\mu)}
as right
\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}
-modules.

Proof.

We have by construction that
\Gamma(X,\mathcal{M}^{\mu}_{n})\underset{R}{\otimes}{}K\cong\Gamma(X_{K},\mathcal{M}^{\mu}_{n,K|X_{K}})
and by
[
32
, Lemma 6.2]
that
\Gamma(X_{K},\mathcal{M}^{\mu}_{n,K|X_{K}})\cong T(\mu)
. Therefore

\Gamma(X,\mathcal{M}^{\mu}_{n})\underset{R}{\otimes}{}K\cong T(\mu)_{0}\underset{R}{\otimes}{}K\cong T(\mu).

By
[
2
, Proposition 5.15b)]
,
\Gamma(X,\mathcal{M}^{\mu}_{n})
is finitely generated as a
U(\mathfrak{g})_{n}
-module, so
\Gamma(X,\mathcal{M}^{\mu}_{n})
and
T(\mu)_{0}
are both lattices for
T(\mu)
. Therefore, they agree modulo bounded
\pi
-torsion, i.e. there exists an exact sequence

T(\mu)_{0}\to\Gamma(X,\mathcal{M}^{\mu}_{n})\to C\to 0,

such that
\pi^{m}C=0
for some
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
C
is a finitely generated
U(\mathfrak{g})_{n}
-module. Since,
\pi
-adic completion is exact on finitely generated
U(\mathfrak{g})_{n}
-modules we get an exact sequence

\widehat{T(\mu)_{0}}\to\widehat{\Gamma(X,\mathcal{M}^{\mu}_{n})}\to\widehat{C}\to 0,

with
\pi^{m}\widehat{C}=0
. So by tensoring with
K
we obtain

\widehat{T(\mu)}=\widehat{T(\mu)_{0}}\underset{R}{\otimes}{}K\cong\widehat{\Gamma(X,\mathcal{M}^{\mu}_{n})}\underset{R}{\otimes}{}K\cong\Gamma(X,\widehat{\mathcal{M}^{\mu}_{n,K}}).\qed

We may now prove the main theorem of this section, an affinoid version of
[
33
, Theorem 6.5]
.

Theorem 6.7
.

Let
\mathcal{M}\in\Coh(\widehat{\mathcal{D}^{\lambda,\mu}_{n,K}},G)
. Then as

U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}^{\lambda}

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

-modules we have:

\Gamma(X,\hat{i}_{l,K}^{\#}\mathcal{M})\cong\widehat{T(\mu)}\underset{\hbox{\hskip 334.65749pt\hskip-12.87434pt\hbox{$U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}^{\mu}$}\hskip-12.87434pt\hskip-334.65749pt\raisebox{7.65pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-0.43054pt}{\resizebox{39724363}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.1425pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68599pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.48999pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.48999pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.34999pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-334.65749pt\hskip 334.65749pt}}{\otimes}{}\Gamma(Z,\mathcal{M}).

Proof.

This follows by combining Corollary
5.8
, Proposition
6.6
and
[
2
, Theorem 6.10b)]
.
∎

7
Affinoid Duflo’s theorem

Throughout this section
\lambda:\pi^{n}\mathfrak{h}\to R
denotes an
R
-linear dominant weight. Recall that we use
\lambda^{*}
to denote the weight
-w_{o}\lambda
, where
w_{o}
is the longest element of the Weyl group. If
\lambda
is a dominant weight, so is
\lambda^{*}
. We consider the functor

\begin{split}&\mathscr{F}:\Mod_{\fg}(\hbox{\hskip 448.9668pt\hskip-24.95215pt\hbox{$U(\mathfrak{{g\times &\mathscr{F}(M):=\Gamma(X,\hat{i}_{l,K}^{\#}\Loc^{\lambda}(M)).\end{split}

Proposition 7.1
.

The functor
\mathscr{F}
is exact and
\mathscr{F}(M)\cong\widehat{T(\lambda)}\underset{\hbox{\hskip 334.65749pt\hskip-12.87434pt\hbox{$U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}^{\lambda}$}\hskip-12.87434pt\hskip-334.65749pt\raisebox{8.82057pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-0.43054pt}{\resizebox{39724363}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.1425pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68599pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.48999pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.48999pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.34999pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-334.65749pt\hskip 334.65749pt}}{\otimes}{}M
as

U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}^{\lambda^{*}}

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

-modules for

Proof.

and
\mathcal{M}:=\Loc^{\lambda^{*},\lambda}(M)
. We have by Corollary

that
\Gamma(Z,\mathcal{M})\cong M
and by Theorem

that
\mathcal{M}\in\Coh(\widehat{\mathcal{D}^{\lambda^{*},\lambda}_{n,K}},G)
, so the second claim follows from Theorem
6.7
. Consider a short exact sequence in

448.9668pt},G)
:

0\to N\to M\to P\to 0.

We let
\mathcal{N},\mathcal{M},\mathcal{P}
denote the localisation of
N,M
and
P
respectively. Further, we denote
\mathcal{L}:=\ker(\mathcal{N}\to\mathcal{M})
. By construction,
\Loc^{\lambda^{*},\lambda}
is right exact, so we obtain an exact sequence:

0\to\mathcal{L}\to\mathcal{N}\to\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{P}\to 0.

Since
\hat{i}_{l,K}^{\#}
is an equivalence of Abelian categories, it is exact. Furthermore, by Theorem
5.8
the global sections functor is also exact, so we obtain an exact sequence

0\to\Gamma(X,\hat{i}_{l,K}^{\#}\mathcal{L})\to\Gamma(X,\hat{i}_{l,K}^{\#}\mathcal{N})\to\Gamma(X,\hat{i}_{l,K}^{\#}\mathcal{M})\to\Gamma(X,\hat{i}_{l,K}^{\#}\mathcal{P})\to 0.

Combining Theorem
6.7
and Corollary
5.9
we obtain an exact sequence

The claim follows since

Lemma 7.2
.

Let
M\in\Mod_{\fg}(\hbox{\hskip 448.9668pt\hskip-24.95215pt\hbox{$U(\mathfrak{{g\times g}})_{n,K}^{\lambda^{*},\lambda}$}\hskip-24.95215pt\hskip-448.9668pt\raisebox{19.97322pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.10002pt}{\resizebox{53331012}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-792.00986pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-792.00986pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resize 448.9668pt},G)
and assume
\mathscr{F}(M)=0
. Then
M=0
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{M}:=\Loc^{\lambda^{*},\lambda}(M)
. Then, by assumption, we have that
\Gamma(X,\hat{i}_{l,K}^{\#}\mathcal{M})=0
. By applying Corollary
5.9
and Corollary
4.22
, we obtain
M=0
.
∎

We now specialise to two sided ideals in

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

via

and

. Further, by Corollary

, we have

g}})_{n,K}^{\lambda^{*},\lambda}$}\hskip-24.95215pt\hskip-448.9668pt\raisebox{19.97322pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.10002pt}{\resizebox{53331012}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-792.00986pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-792.00986pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resize 448.9668pt},G)
, so
\mathscr{F}(I)
is well-defined. As a corollary, we obtain immediately:

Corollary 7.3
.

Let
I,J
be two-sided ideals in

U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}^{\lambda}

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

such that
I\subseteq J
. Assume that
\mathscr{F}(I)\cong\mathscr{F}(J)
. Then
I=J
.

Proof.

Consider the short exact sequence:

0\to I\to J\to J/I\to 0.

By Proposition
7.1
the functor
\mathscr{F}
is exact, so we obtain an exact sequence

0\to\mathscr{F}(I)\to\mathscr{F}(J)\to\mathscr{F}(J/I)\to 0.

Using the assumption, we obtain
\mathscr{F}(J/I)=0
. The claim follows by Lemma
7.2
.
∎

Corollary 7.4
.

Let
I
be a two-sided ideal in

U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}^{\lambda}

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

. Then as left

U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}^{\lambda^{*}}

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

-modules we have

\mathscr{F}(I)\cong\widehat{T(\lambda)}I.

We should remark that

U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}^{\lambda^{*}}

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

acts on
\widehat{T(\lambda)}I
via
x.(ti)=t(x.i)=ti\tau(x)
for
x\in\hbox{\hskip 448.9668pt\hskip-17.80641pt\hbox{$U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}^{\lambda^{*}}$}\hskip-17.80641pt\hskip-448.9668pt\raisebox{19.97322pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.10002pt}{\resizebox{53331012}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-792.00986pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-792.00986pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-448.9668pt\hskip 448.9668pt},t\in\widehat{T(\lambda)}
and
i\in I
. Further, the isomorphism is natural in
I
.

Proof.

334.65749pt\hskip-12.87434pt\hbox{$U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}^{\lambda}$}\hskip-12.87434pt\hskip-334.65749pt\raisebox{8.82057pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-0.43054pt}{\resizebox{39724363}{98759}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-186.48691pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{42775755}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-186.48691pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{42775755}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-186.48708pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{42775755}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68599pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.48999pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-186.48691pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{42775755}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0. mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-448.9668pt\hskip 448.9668pt}/I}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}

It is easy to see that the first map is a surjection and the second and the third maps are isomorphisms. Furthermore, by the diagram above, the first map is also injective, so indeed we get
\mathscr{F}(I)\cong\widehat{T(\lambda)}I
.
∎

Let
\sigma:\mathfrak{g}\to\mathfrak{g}
denote the Chevalley involution that swaps
\mathfrak{n}^{+}
and
\mathfrak{n}^{-}
and fixes
\mathfrak{h}
. Then
\sigma
extends to an anti-automorphism of
U(\mathfrak{g})_{K}
that fixes the center by
[
22
, Exercise 1.10]
. Therefore, we obtain that an anti-automorphism
\hat{\sigma}:\hbox{\hskip 448.9668pt\hskip-17.80641pt\hbox{$U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}^{\lambda}$}\hskip-17.80641pt\hskip-448.9668pt\raisebox{19.49014pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.10002pt}{\resizebox{53331012}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-792.00986pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-792.00986pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-448.9668pt\hskip 448.9668pt}\to\hbox{\hskip 448.9668pt\hskip-17.80641pt\hbox{$U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}^{\lambda}$}\hskip-17.80641pt\hskip-448.9668pt\raisebox{19.49014pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.10002pt}{\resizebox{53331012}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-792.00986pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-792.00986pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-448.9668pt\hskip 448.9668pt}
. Recall that
T(\lambda)_{0}=R_{\lambda}\underset{U(\mathfrak{b}^{-})_{n}}{\otimes}{}U(\mathfrak{g})
, so that

\widehat{T(\lambda)}\cong K_{\lambda}\underset{\widehat{U(\mathfrak{b}^{-})_{n,K}}}{\otimes}{}\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}
.

Lemma 7.5
.

The map

\hat{\phi}:\widehat{T(\lambda)}\to\widehat{M(\lambda)},\qquad\hat{\phi}(k\otimes x)=\hat{\sigma}(x)\otimes k,\qquad k\in K_{\lambda},x\in\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}

is a
K
-linear isomorphism of vector spaces satisfying
\hat{\phi}(tu)=\hat{\sigma}(u)\hat{\phi}(t)
for all
u\in\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}
and
t\in\widehat{T(\lambda)}
. In particular, if
I
is a two-sided ideal in

U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}^{\lambda}

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

, then
\hat{\phi}(\widehat{T(\lambda)}I)=\hat{\sigma}(I)\widehat{M(\lambda)}
.

Proof.

We have by
[
32
, Lemma 7.6]
that the map
\phi:T(\lambda)\to M(\lambda)
,
\phi(k\otimes x)=\sigma(x)\otimes K
is a
K
-linear isomorphism of vector spaces satisfying
\phi(tu)=\sigma(u)\phi(t)
for all
t\in T(\lambda)
and
u\in U(\mathfrak{g})_{K}
. Therefore, the claims follow from the construction of
\hat{\phi}
and
\hat{\sigma}
.
∎

To prove the main theorem, we will need the following corollary:

Corollary 7.6
.

Let
I
be a two-sided ideal in

U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}^{\lambda}

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

. Then:

I=\Ann(\widehat{M(\lambda)}/I\widehat{M(\lambda)}).

Proof.

Let
J:=\Ann(\widehat{M(\lambda)}/I\widehat{M(\lambda)})
. Since
I(\widehat{M(\lambda)}/I\widehat{M(\lambda)})=0
, we obtain that
I\subseteq J
, so
\hat{\sigma}(I)\subseteq\hat{\sigma}(J)
. We remark that since
\hat{\sigma}
is an anti-automorphism of

U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}^{\lambda}

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

,
\hat{\sigma}(I)
and
\hat{\sigma}(J)
are also two-sided ideals in

U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}^{\lambda}

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

. Since
\mathscr{F}
is exact, in particular left exact, we obtain
\mathscr{F}(\hat{\sigma}(I))\subseteq\mathscr{F}(\hat{\sigma}(J))
. Consider the following diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{F}(\hat{\sigma}(I))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{F}(\hat{\sigma}(J))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{T(\lambda)}\hat{\sigma}(I)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{T(\lambda)}\hat{\sigma}(J)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I\widehat{M(\lambda)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces J\widehat{M(\lambda)}\par}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\phi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\phi}}

By construction, the bottom diagram commutes and by the definition of
J
, we have
J\widehat{M(\lambda)}=I\widehat{M(\lambda)}
. Using Lemma
7.5
, we get
\widehat{T(\lambda)}\hat{\sigma}(I)=\widehat{T(\lambda)}\hat{\sigma}(J)
. Furthermore, since the isomorphism is Lemma

7.4
is natural on ideals, the top diagram is also commutative. Therefore,
\mathscr{F}(\hat{\sigma}(I))\cong\mathscr{F}(\hat{\sigma}(J))
. The claim follows from Corollary
7.3
since
\hat{\sigma}
is an anti-automorphism.
∎

Theorem 7.7
.

Let
I
be a prime ideal in

U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}^{\lambda}

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

. Then

I=\Ann(\widehat{L(\mu)})\text{ for some }\mu:\pi^{n}\mathfrak{h}\to R.

Proof.

Since
\widehat{M(\lambda)}/I\widehat{M(\lambda)}
is a quotient of
\widehat{M(\lambda)}
, we have by Proposition
3.29
that there exists a finite composition series:

0=M_{0}\subset M_{1}\subset\ldots M_{l}=\widehat{M(\lambda)}/I\widehat{M(\lambda)}.

Let
I_{i}=\Ann(M_{i}/M_{i-1})
for
1\leq i\leq l
. We have

I_{1}I_{2}\ldots I_{l}M_{l}=I_{1}I_{2}\ldots I_{l-1}M_{l-1}=\ldots=0,

so
I_{1}I_{2}\ldots I_{l}\subset\Ann(\widehat{M(\lambda)}/I\widehat{M(\lambda)})=I
by Corollary
7.6
. Since
I
is prime, there exists
1\leq j\leq l
such that
I_{j}\subset I
. On the other hand, we have by construction
I\subset I_{j}
, so
I=I_{j}=\Ann(M_{j}/M_{j-1})
. Finally, we have by Proposition
3.29
that
M_{j}/M_{j-1}\cong\widehat{L(\mu)}
for some
\mu:\pi^{n}\mathfrak{h}\to R
.
∎

As an easy corollary, we obtain a positive answer to
[
3
, Question A]
.

Corollary 7.8
.

Every primitive ideal of
\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}
with
K
-rational
infinitesimal central character is the annihilator of a simple affinoid highest weight module.

Proof.

Any primitive ideal in
\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}
with
K
-rational infinitesimal central character intersects
Z(\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}})
in a maximal ideal of the form
\ker\chi_{\lambda}
; here we view
\ker\chi_{\lambda}
as a central character of
\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}
via Theorem
3.4
. Therefore, classifying these ideals reduces to classifying the ideals in

U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}^{\lambda}

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

for all
\lambda\in\pi^{n}\mathfrak{h}^{*}
.
There is an action of the Weyl group
W
on the set of weights such that for two weights
\lambda
and
\mu
,
\hbox{\hskip 448.9668pt\hskip-17.80641pt\hbox{$U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}^{\lambda}$}\hskip-17.80641pt\hskip-448.9668pt\raisebox{19.49014pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.10002pt}{\resizebox{53331012}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-792.00986pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-792.00986pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-448.9668pt\hskip 448.9668pt}=\widehat{U(\mathfrak{g})^{\mu}_{n,K}}
if and only if
\lambda
and
\mu
are
W
-conjugate. Further, every
W
-conjugacy class contains at least one dominant weight. The claim follows by Theorem
7.7
since every primitive ideal is prime.
∎

We should remark that in the case
p
is a very good prime for
G
, we have by Theorem
3.6
that the ideals
\{\hat{I}_{\lambda}=\Ann(\widehat{M(\lambda)})|\lambda\in\pi^{n}\mathfrak{h}^{*}\}
form the set of minimal primitive ideals with
K
-rational central character.

8
A controller theorem

We keep the notations and assumptions from the previous section; we further assume that
\lambda:\pi^{n}\mathfrak{h}\to R
is also
regular
. We will also make use of the fact that two-sided ideals in
\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}
that contain
\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}\ker{\chi_{\lambda}}
correspond to two-sided ideals in

U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}^{\lambda}

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

.

Lemma 8.1
.

Let
I
be a two-sided ideal in
U(\mathfrak{g}_{K})
. Then
\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}I
is a two-sided ideal in
\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}I
and furthermore,
\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}I=\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}I\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}
.

Proof.

Clearly, it is enough to prove that
I\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}\subset\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}I.

Viewing
\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}
as a left
\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}
-module via left multiplication, we have that
\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}I
is a
\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}
-submodule. Since the topology
\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}
is complete, we have by
[
21
, I.5.5]
that
\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}I
is a closed subset.

Let
i\in I
and
x\in\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}
. Recall that assuming that
u_{1},u_{2}\ldots u_{d}
is a free
R
-basis for
\mathfrak{g}
, we may write as
x=\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{d}}c_{\alpha}u^{\alpha}
, with
||p^{-n}c_{\alpha}||\to 0
as
|\alpha|\to\infty
. For
k\in\mathbb{N}
, let
x_{k}=\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{d},|\alpha|\leq k}c_{\alpha}u^{\alpha}
. Since
I
is a two-sided ideal, we obtain that
ix_{k}\in I\subset\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}I
.

Finally, we have
ix=i\lim_{k\to\infty}x_{k}=\lim_{k\to\infty}ix_{k}
. Since
\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}I
is closed, we obtain
ix\in\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}I
finishing the proof.
∎

Proposition 8.2
.

Let
I
be a two-sided ideal in
\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}
such that
\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}\ker{\chi_{\lambda}}\subset I
. Then there exists a two-sided ideal
J
in
U(\mathfrak{g}_{K})
such that
I=\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}J
.

Proof.

Let
\hat{M}=I\widehat{M(\lambda)}.
We have by Theorem
3.25
that there exists
M
a submodule of
M(\lambda)
such that
\hat{M}=\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}.M
. Further, we have by
[
8
, Theorem 4.3]
that there exists
J
a two-sided ideal
U(\mathfrak{g}_{K})
such that
JM(\lambda)=M
. By applying Lemma
8.1
we obtain:

\begin{split}(\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}J).\widehat{M(\lambda)}&=(\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}J)\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}.M(\lambda)\\
&=(\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}J).M(\lambda)=\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}.M=\hat{M},\end{split}

(44)

so
(\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}J)\widehat{M(\lambda)}=I\widehat{M(\lambda)}
. Let
J^{\prime}=\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}J+I
, so that
J^{\prime}\widehat{M(\lambda)}=I\widehat{M(\lambda)}
and
I\subset J^{\prime}
. We have by Lemma
8.1
that
J^{\prime}
is also a two-sided ideal. Further, by combining Corollary
7.3
, Corollary
7.4
and Lemma
7.5
we get
J^{\prime}=I
, so
\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}J\subset I
. Applying the same strategy again, we obtain
\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}J=I
.
∎

To finish the proof, we need one more lemma. This is probably well-known among the experts, but we have not been able to locate a reference.

Lemma 8.3
.

Let
S\subset T
two rings and let
I
be a left ideal of
T
generated by
X\subset S
. Then
I=T(I\cap S)
.

Proof.

Let
J=I\cap S
. Obviously, we have
TJ\subset I
. On the other hand, we have
X\subset S
,
X\subset I
, so
X\subset J
. Therefore,
I=T.X\subset TJ
. The claim follows.
∎

Theorem 8.4
.

Let
\lambda:\pi^{n}\mathfrak{h}\to R
be a
R
-linear dominant regular weight. Let
I
be a two-sided ideal in
\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}
with
\chi_{\lambda}
-central character. Then:

I=\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}(I\cap U(\mathfrak{g}_{K})).

Proof.

This follows immediately from Proposition
8.2
and Lemma
8.3
.
∎

As a corollary, we obtain immediately:

Corollary 8.5
.

Let
\lambda:\pi^{n}\mathfrak{h}\to R
be a
R
-linear dominant regular weight. The maps:

\begin{split}I&\mapsto I\cap U(\mathfrak{g}_{K})\\
J&\mapsto\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}J\end{split}

(45)

induce inverse bijections between the set of two sided ideals in
\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}
with
\chi_{\lambda}
central character and the set of two sided ideals in
\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}
with
\chi_{\lambda}
central character.

We may also prove which ideal controls the annihilator of the simple affinoid module
\widehat{L(\mu)}
.

Proposition 8.6
.

Let
\mu:\pi^{n}\mathfrak{h}\to R
and assume that
\mu
is
W
-linked to
\lambda
. Then:

\Ann(\widehat{L(\mu)})=\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}\Ann L(\mu).

Proof.

Let
\hat{P}:=\Ann(\widehat{L(\mu)})
,
P=\Ann(L(\mu))
and
J=\hat{P}\cap U(\mathfrak{g}_{K})
. Then, we have by construction
P\subset J
.

We claim that
\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}P
contains the annihilator of
\widehat{L(\mu)}
. We have by the proof of Lemma y
8.1
that for all
p\in P
and
x\in\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}
, there exist
q\in P
and
y\in\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}
such that
px=yq
. Therefore, we have for all
z\in\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}
and
x\otimes l\in\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}\underset{U(\mathfrak{g}_{K})}{\otimes}{}L
that

zp.(x\otimes l)=z(px\otimes l)=zy(q\otimes l)=zy(1\otimes q.l)=0,

so the claim is proven. Therefore, we obtain using Corollary
8.5
that
P=J
and so,
\hat{P}=\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}P
.
∎

8.1
Characterisation of all two-sided ideals

We may now use an affinoid version of Quillen’s Lemma to prove that a large class of two-sided ideals in
\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}
is controlled by two-sided ideals in the classical enveloping algebra
U(\mathfrak{g})_{K}
.

We are now able to characterise all the primitive ideals in
\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}
for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

Theorem 8.7
.

Let
I
be a primitive ideal in
\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}
. Then there exists a finite extension
L/K
and a primitive ideal
J\in\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}\underset{K}{\otimes}{}L
with
L
-rational central character such that:

I=J\cap\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}.

Further, this ideal
J
is of the form
\Ann(\widehat{L(\lambda)})
for some suitable
\lambda
.

Proof.

We have by Theorem
3.4
that
Z(\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}})
is isomorphic with a Tate algebra. Further, we have by
[
34
, Theorem 6.4.6]
that
I
has some central character. Therefore, there exists
L/K
finite extension such that
I\underset{K}{\otimes}{}L\subset\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}\underset{R}{\otimes}{}K
has
L
-rational central character.

Let
e
be the ramification index of
L/K
,
\mathcal{O}_{L}
the ring of integers of
L
,
\pi^{\prime}
the uniformiser of
\mathcal{O}_{L}
and
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}=\mathfrak{g}\underset{R}{\otimes}{}\mathcal{O}_{L}
. We have by
[
2
, Lemma 3.9 c)]
that
\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}\otimes L\cong\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g^{\prime}}})_{en,L}}
. Finally, we have by
[
25
, Theorem 10.2.9]
that there exists a prime ideal
J\in\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g^{\prime}}})_{en,L}}
such that
I=J\cap\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}
. Thus
J
contains
I\underset{K}{\otimes}{}L
, so in particular it has an
L
-rational central character. The claim follows from Theorem
7.7
.
∎

Theorem 8.8
.

Let
I
be a two-sided ideal in
\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}
and assume it has a central character
\chi:Z(\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}})\to\overline{K}
generated by a dominant regular weight. Then
I
is controlled by a two-sided ideal in
U(\mathfrak{g})_{K}
.

Proof.

We have by the proof of Theorem
8.7
that there exists a finite extension
L/K
such that
I\underset{K}{\otimes}{}L
has a
L
-rational central character. By passing to the Galois closure, if necessary, we may assume that
L/K
is a Galois extension; let
\mathcal{G}=\Gal(L/K)
denote the corresponding Galois group. Again, let
e
be the ramification index of
L/K
,
\mathcal{O}_{L}
the ring of integers of
L
,
\pi^{\prime}
the uniformiser of
\mathcal{O}_{L}
and
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}=\mathfrak{g}\underset{R}{\otimes}{}\mathcal{O}_{L}
. As before, it follows from
[
2
, Lemma 3.9 c)]
that
\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}\underset{K}{\otimes}{}L\cong\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g^{\prime}}})_{en,L}}
.

Since
I
is a two-sided ideal in
\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}
and
L/K
is finite, we get that
I\underset{K}{\otimes}{}L
is a two-sided ideal in
\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}\underset{K}{\otimes}{}L\cong\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g^{\prime}}})_{en,L}}
. Therefore, we have by Proposition
8.2
that there exists
J
, a two-sided ideal in
U(\mathfrak{g^{\prime}})_{L}
, such that
I\underset{K}{\otimes}{}L=\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g^{\prime}}})_{en,L}}J
.

By construction,
I\underset{K}{\otimes}{}L
is closed under the natural action of
\mathcal{G}
, therefore
J
is also closed under the corresponding
\mathcal{G}
-action on
U(\mathfrak{g^{\prime}})_{L}
. We have

U(\mathfrak{g^{\prime}})_{L}\cong U(\mathfrak{g}\underset{R}{\otimes}{}\mathcal{O}_{L})\underset{\mathcal{O}_{L}}{\otimes}{}L\cong U(\mathfrak{g})\underset{R}{\otimes}{}\mathcal{O}_{L}\underset{\mathcal{O}_{L}}{\otimes}{}L\cong U(\mathfrak{g})\underset{R}{\otimes}{}L\cong U(\mathfrak{g})_{K}\underset{K}{\otimes}{}L.

(46)

Since
L/K
is a finite Galois extension, we have by Galois descent and the same arguments as in
[
15
, Theorem 4.2]
that
J=J_{0}U(\mathfrak{g^{\prime}})_{L}
for some two-sided ideal
J_{0}
in
U(\mathfrak{g})_{K}
. We have

I\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g^{\prime}}})_{en,L}}=I\underset{K}{\otimes}{}L=J\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g^{\prime}}})_{en,L}}=(J_{0}U(\mathfrak{g^{\prime}})_{L})\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g^{\prime}}})_{en,L}}=J_{0}\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g^{\prime}}})_{en,L}}=(J_{0}\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}})\underset{K}{\otimes}{}L.

(47)

Therefore, by taking
\mathcal{G}
-invariants and taking into account that
I
and
J_{0}\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}
are fixed by the
\mathcal{G}
-action, we obtain

I=(I\underset{K}{\otimes}{}L)^{\mathcal{G}}=((J_{0}\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}})\underset{K}{\otimes}{}L)^{\mathcal{G}}=J_{0}\widehat{U(\mathfrak{{g}})_{n,K}}.

(48)

In conclusion,
I
is controlled by
J_{0}
and the claim is proven.
∎
